######################################################
# FILE: .github/actions/android-keystore/action.yml
######################################################

name: Prepare Android signing keystore
description: >
  Materialise the release keystore from repository secrets and verify it is the
  key we expect before any signing happens. Fails loudly rather than letting a
  build fall through to producing an unsigned artifact.

# Accepts the keystore in either of the two shapes the secrets can hold:
#
#   1. A whole keystore in KEYSTORE_RAW — base64-encoded (what
#      `base64 -w0 upload.jks` produces) or the raw bytes. JKS, JCEKS and
#      PKCS#12 are all detected from their magic bytes, so it doesn't matter
#      which one was uploaded.
#   2. The key material split across KEYSTORE_PRIVATE / KEYSTORE_RSA (private
#      key) plus KEYSTORE_PUBLIC (signing certificate) and optionally
#      KEYSTORE_CHAIN (the rest of the chain), in PEM or base64-wrapped PEM.
#      These are assembled into a PKCS#12 keystore with openssl + keytool.
#
# Whichever path is used, the result is checked against KEYSTORE_SHA256 /
# KEYSTORE_SHA1 / KEYSTORE_OWNER when those secrets are set. A keystore that
# doesn't contain the alias, or whose certificate fingerprint doesn't match,
# fails the job here — before a wrong-key artifact can be published, which on
# Android is unrecoverable for anyone who already installed the app.

inputs:
  keystore-raw:
    description: KEYSTORE_RAW — a complete keystore, base64-encoded or raw.
    required: false
    default: ''
  keystore-private:
    description: KEYSTORE_PRIVATE — private key (PEM or base64-wrapped PEM).
    required: false
    default: ''
  keystore-rsa:
    description: KEYSTORE_RSA — alternative private key source, tried if KEYSTORE_PRIVATE is empty.
    required: false
    default: ''
  keystore-public:
    description: KEYSTORE_PUBLIC — the signing certificate (PEM or base64-wrapped PEM).
    required: false
    default: ''
  keystore-chain:
    description: KEYSTORE_CHAIN — any intermediate/root certificates (PEM).
    required: false
    default: ''
  keystore-password:
    description: KEYSTORE_PASSWORD — the keystore (store) password.
    required: true
  key-alias:
    description: KEY_ALIAS — the alias of the signing key inside the keystore.
    required: true
  key-password:
    description: KEY_PASSWORD — the key password. Defaults to the store password when empty.
    required: false
    default: ''
  expected-sha256:
    description: KEYSTORE_SHA256 — expected certificate SHA-256 fingerprint. Checked when set.
    required: false
    default: ''
  expected-sha1:
    description: KEYSTORE_SHA1 — expected certificate SHA-1 fingerprint. Checked when set.
    required: false
    default: ''
  expected-owner:
    description: KEYSTORE_OWNER — expected certificate Owner DN. Checked when set.
    required: false
    default: ''

outputs:
  keystore-path:
    description: Absolute path to the prepared keystore file.
    value: ${{ steps.prepare.outputs.keystore-path }}
  store-type:
    description: JKS, JCEKS or PKCS12 — pass to Gradle as QUICLOC_KEYSTORE_TYPE.
    value: ${{ steps.prepare.outputs.store-type }}
  sha256:
    description: The SHA-256 fingerprint of the certificate that will sign the build.
    value: ${{ steps.prepare.outputs.sha256 }}

runs:
  using: composite
  steps:
    - id: prepare
      shell: bash
      env:
        KEYSTORE_RAW: ${{ inputs.keystore-raw }}
        KEYSTORE_PRIVATE: ${{ inputs.keystore-private }}
        KEYSTORE_RSA: ${{ inputs.keystore-rsa }}
        KEYSTORE_PUBLIC: ${{ inputs.keystore-public }}
        KEYSTORE_CHAIN: ${{ inputs.keystore-chain }}
        KEYSTORE_PASSWORD: ${{ inputs.keystore-password }}
        KEY_ALIAS: ${{ inputs.key-alias }}
        KEY_PASSWORD: ${{ inputs.key-password }}
        EXPECTED_SHA256: ${{ inputs.expected-sha256 }}
        EXPECTED_SHA1: ${{ inputs.expected-sha1 }}
        EXPECTED_OWNER: ${{ inputs.expected-owner }}
      run: |
        set -euo pipefail

        work="$RUNNER_TEMP/signing"
        mkdir -p "$work"
        chmod 700 "$work"

        if [ -z "${KEYSTORE_PASSWORD:-}" ]; then
          echo "::error::KEYSTORE_PASSWORD secret is empty or unset. Set it to the keystore (store) password."
          exit 1
        fi
        if [ -z "${KEY_ALIAS:-}" ]; then
          echo "::error::KEY_ALIAS secret is empty or unset. Set it to the alias of the signing key."
          exit 1
        fi
        # A keystore created by `keytool -genkeypair` without -keypass uses the
        # store password for the key too, which is the common case.
        if [ -z "${KEY_PASSWORD:-}" ]; then
          echo "KEY_PASSWORD is empty — using the store password for the key as well."
          KEY_PASSWORD="$KEYSTORE_PASSWORD"
        fi
        export KEYSTORE_PASSWORD KEY_PASSWORD

        # Write a secret to a file, tolerating either raw bytes or base64. PEM
        # is recognised directly; everything else is attempted as base64 first
        # (that is how a binary keystore has to travel through a secret) and
        # falls back to the literal bytes. Returns 1 when there was nothing to
        # write, so callers can branch on "was this secret set?".
        materialise() {
          local value="$1" out="$2"
          [ -n "$value" ] || return 1
          # Strip surrounding whitespace/newlines that copy-paste tends to add.
          printf '%s' "$value" | sed -e 's/^[[:space:]]*//' -e 's/[[:space:]]*$//' > "$work/.in"
          if head -c 11 "$work/.in" | grep -q -- "-----BEGIN"; then
            cp "$work/.in" "$out"
          elif tr -d '[:space:]' < "$work/.in" | base64 --decode > "$out" 2>/dev/null && [ -s "$out" ]; then
            : # decoded fine
          else
            cp "$work/.in" "$out"
          fi
          rm -f "$work/.in"
          [ -s "$out" ]
        }

        # Keystore format from its first bytes: JKS and JCEKS have their own
        # magic numbers, PKCS#12 is a DER SEQUENCE. `od` rather than `xxd` —
        # coreutils is always there, vim-common isn't.
        detect_store_type() {
          local f="$1" magic
          magic=$(head -c 4 "$f" | od -An -tx1 | tr -d ' \n' || true)
          case "$magic" in
            feedfeed*) echo JKS ;;
            cececece*) echo JCEKS ;;
            3082*)     echo PKCS12 ;;
            *)         echo UNKNOWN ;;
          esac
        }

        keystore="$work/release.keystore"
        store_type=""

        # ---- Path 1: a complete keystore in KEYSTORE_RAW --------------------
        if materialise "${KEYSTORE_RAW:-}" "$keystore"; then
          store_type=$(detect_store_type "$keystore")
          if [ "$store_type" = "UNKNOWN" ]; then
            echo "::warning::KEYSTORE_RAW did not decode to a recognisable keystore (JKS/JCEKS/PKCS12). Falling back to assembling one from the key + certificate secrets."
            rm -f "$keystore"
            store_type=""
          else
            echo "Using the keystore from KEYSTORE_RAW (detected $store_type)."
          fi
        fi

        # ---- Path 2: assemble one from private key + certificate ------------
        if [ -z "$store_type" ]; then
          key_pem="$work/key.pem"
          cert_pem="$work/cert.pem"
          chain_pem="$work/chain.pem"

          if ! materialise "${KEYSTORE_PRIVATE:-}" "$key_pem"; then
            materialise "${KEYSTORE_RSA:-}" "$key_pem" || true
          fi
          if [ ! -s "$key_pem" ]; then
            echo "::error::No usable keystore. KEYSTORE_RAW was empty or unrecognisable, and neither KEYSTORE_PRIVATE nor KEYSTORE_RSA contains a private key."
            exit 1
          fi
          # A DER-encoded key still works if we convert it to PEM first.
          if ! grep -q -- "-----BEGIN" "$key_pem"; then
            openssl pkey -inform DER -in "$key_pem" -out "$work/key.converted.pem" 2>/dev/null \
              || openssl rsa -inform DER -in "$key_pem" -out "$work/key.converted.pem" 2>/dev/null \
              || { echo "::error::The private key secret is neither PEM nor DER — could not read it."; exit 1; }
            mv "$work/key.converted.pem" "$key_pem"
          fi

          if ! materialise "${KEYSTORE_PUBLIC:-}" "$cert_pem"; then
            materialise "${KEYSTORE_CHAIN:-}" "$cert_pem" || {
              echo "::error::A private key was supplied but no certificate. Set KEYSTORE_PUBLIC (the signing certificate) as well."
              exit 1
            }
          fi

          p12="$work/assembled.p12"
          extra_certs=()
          if materialise "${KEYSTORE_CHAIN:-}" "$chain_pem" && ! cmp -s "$chain_pem" "$cert_pem"; then
            extra_certs=(-certfile "$chain_pem")
          fi

          echo "Assembling a PKCS#12 keystore from the key + certificate secrets."
          openssl pkcs12 -export \
            -inkey "$key_pem" \
            -in "$cert_pem" \
            "${extra_certs[@]}" \
            -name "$KEY_ALIAS" \
            -passout env:KEYSTORE_PASSWORD \
            -out "$p12"

          # Re-import so the key entry carries KEY_PASSWORD when it differs
          # from the store password (openssl can only set one passphrase).
          keytool -importkeystore \
            -srckeystore "$p12" -srcstoretype PKCS12 -srcstorepass:env KEYSTORE_PASSWORD \
            -srcalias "$KEY_ALIAS" \
            -destkeystore "$keystore" -deststoretype PKCS12 \
            -deststorepass:env KEYSTORE_PASSWORD -destkeypass:env KEY_PASSWORD \
            -destalias "$KEY_ALIAS" -noprompt
          rm -f "$p12" "$key_pem"
          store_type=PKCS12
        fi

        chmod 600 "$keystore"

        # ---- Verify it is the key we think it is ---------------------------
        if ! keytool -list -v -keystore "$keystore" -storetype "$store_type" \
              -storepass:env KEYSTORE_PASSWORD -alias "$KEY_ALIAS" > "$work/cert.txt" 2>"$work/cert.err"; then
          echo "::error::The keystore does not contain the alias '$KEY_ALIAS', or KEYSTORE_PASSWORD is wrong."
          echo "keytool said:"; sed -e "s/$KEY_ALIAS/<alias>/g" "$work/cert.err" || true
          echo "Aliases actually present:"
          keytool -list -keystore "$keystore" -storetype "$store_type" -storepass:env KEYSTORE_PASSWORD \
            | grep -i "PrivateKeyEntry\|trustedCertEntry" || true
          exit 1
        fi

        norm() { tr -d ': \t' | tr '[:lower:]' '[:upper:]'; }
        actual_sha256=$(grep -m1 'SHA256:' "$work/cert.txt" | sed 's/.*SHA256: *//' | tr -d '\r')
        actual_sha1=$(grep -m1 'SHA1:' "$work/cert.txt" | sed 's/.*SHA1: *//' | tr -d '\r')
        actual_owner=$(grep -m1 '^Owner:' "$work/cert.txt" | sed 's/^Owner: *//' | tr -d '\r')

        echo "Signing certificate"
        echo "  Owner:  $actual_owner"
        echo "  SHA-1:  $actual_sha1"
        echo "  SHA-256:$actual_sha256"

        fail=0
        if [ -n "${EXPECTED_SHA256:-}" ] && \
           [ "$(printf '%s' "$actual_sha256" | norm)" != "$(printf '%s' "$EXPECTED_SHA256" | norm)" ]; then
          echo "::error::Certificate SHA-256 does not match the KEYSTORE_SHA256 secret. This is the wrong signing key — publishing with it would break upgrades for every existing install."
          echo "  expected: $EXPECTED_SHA256"
          echo "  actual:   $actual_sha256"
          fail=1
        fi
        if [ -n "${EXPECTED_SHA1:-}" ] && \
           [ "$(printf '%s' "$actual_sha1" | norm)" != "$(printf '%s' "$EXPECTED_SHA1" | norm)" ]; then
          echo "::error::Certificate SHA-1 does not match the KEYSTORE_SHA1 secret."
          echo "  expected: $EXPECTED_SHA1"
          echo "  actual:   $actual_sha1"
          fail=1
        fi
        if [ -n "${EXPECTED_OWNER:-}" ] && [ "$actual_owner" != "$EXPECTED_OWNER" ]; then
          # A DN mismatch is worth flagging but is not proof of the wrong key —
          # the fingerprints are the authoritative check.
          echo "::warning::Certificate Owner does not match the KEYSTORE_OWNER secret (expected '$EXPECTED_OWNER', got '$actual_owner')."
        fi
        [ "$fail" -eq 0 ] || exit 1

        {
          echo "keystore-path=$keystore"
          echo "store-type=$store_type"
          echo "sha256=$actual_sha256"
        } >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"

        rm -f "$work/cert.err"
        echo "Keystore ready ($store_type), alias '$KEY_ALIAS' verified."


######################################################
# FILE: .github/actions/build-universal-apk/action.yml
######################################################

name: Build a fused universal APK from an AAB
description: >
  Turn a signed .aab into a single installable .apk that carries every
  dist:fusing-marked dynamic feature module, via bundletool's universal mode.

# Why this exists: `./gradlew assembleRelease` on the base app module ONLY
# builds the base module's own code — Android Gradle Plugin does not fold
# dynamic-feature modules into it, regardless of `dist:fusing` in the
# feature's manifest. That attribute is read by bundletool, not by
# `assembleRelease`. So the plain base APK that used to be attached straight
# to GitHub Releases silently shipped with zero find-my-phone/lockdown code
# for every sideload user, even while the feature was fully wired up and
# `FindMyPhone.ENABLED` was true. This action is the fix: build the AAB
# (which DOES contain every module — `bundleRelease` already worked
# correctly), then ask bundletool for a "universal" split — one APK with
# every dist:fusing module folded in — and sign it with the same release key
# Gradle would have used.

inputs:
  aab-path:
    description: Path to the signed .aab produced by `bundleRelease`.
    required: true
  keystore-path:
    description: Path to the release keystore (same one Gradle signed the AAB with).
    required: true
  keystore-password:
    description: Keystore (store) password.
    required: true
  key-alias:
    description: Alias of the signing key inside the keystore.
    required: true
  key-password:
    description: Key password. Defaults to the store password when empty.
    required: false
    default: ''
  bundletool-version:
    description: Pinned bundletool release version to download.
    required: false
    default: '1.18.1'
  output-path:
    description: Where to write the resulting universal .apk.
    required: true

outputs:
  apk-path:
    description: Path to the fused, signed universal .apk (same as output-path).
    value: ${{ steps.build.outputs.apk-path }}

runs:
  using: composite
  steps:
    - id: build
      shell: bash
      env:
        AAB_PATH: ${{ inputs.aab-path }}
        KEYSTORE_PATH: ${{ inputs.keystore-path }}
        KEYSTORE_PASSWORD: ${{ inputs.keystore-password }}
        KEY_ALIAS: ${{ inputs.key-alias }}
        KEY_PASSWORD: ${{ inputs.key-password }}
        BUNDLETOOL_VERSION: ${{ inputs.bundletool-version }}
        OUTPUT_PATH: ${{ inputs.output-path }}
      run: |
        set -euo pipefail

        if [ ! -f "$AAB_PATH" ]; then
          echo "::error::No .aab found at $AAB_PATH — run bundleRelease first."
          exit 1
        fi
        if [ -z "${KEY_PASSWORD:-}" ]; then
          KEY_PASSWORD="$KEYSTORE_PASSWORD"
        fi

        work="$RUNNER_TEMP/bundletool"
        mkdir -p "$work"

        jar="$work/bundletool.jar"
        url="https://github.com/google/bundletool/releases/download/${BUNDLETOOL_VERSION}/bundletool-all-${BUNDLETOOL_VERSION}.jar"
        echo "Downloading bundletool ${BUNDLETOOL_VERSION}..."
        curl -sSL -o "$jar" "$url"
        if [ ! -s "$jar" ]; then
          echo "::error::Failed to download bundletool from $url"
          exit 1
        fi

        apks="$work/app.apks"
        rm -f "$apks"
        java -jar "$jar" build-apks \
          --bundle="$AAB_PATH" \
          --output="$apks" \
          --mode=universal \
          --ks="$KEYSTORE_PATH" \
          --ks-pass="pass:$KEYSTORE_PASSWORD" \
          --ks-key-alias="$KEY_ALIAS" \
          --key-pass="pass:$KEY_PASSWORD"

        mkdir -p "$(dirname "$OUTPUT_PATH")"
        # `build-apks --mode=universal` writes a .apks zip containing exactly
        # one entry, universal.apk, at its root — already signed with the
        # keystore we just passed it, no separate apksigner step needed.
        unzip -p "$apks" universal.apk > "$OUTPUT_PATH"
        if [ ! -s "$OUTPUT_PATH" ]; then
          echo "::error::bundletool produced no universal.apk inside $apks"
          exit 1
        fi

        echo "apk-path=$OUTPUT_PATH" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
        echo "Universal APK written to $OUTPUT_PATH ($(du -h "$OUTPUT_PATH" | cut -f1))"


######################################################
# FILE: .github/actions/play-api-token/action.yml
######################################################

name: Mint a Google Play API access token
description: >
  Exchange the service-account JSON for an androidpublisher OAuth token, and
  write it to a file. Used by both the preflight and the publish step.

# Self-contained: jq + openssl + curl, all present on ubuntu-latest. No
# third-party action gets to see the key.
#
# The token lands in a file rather than a step output because outputs are
# easy to leak into logs by accident, and this is a bearer credential for the
# app's Play listing. It is also masked, belt and braces.
#
# Tokens are minted with a 5-minute life. Mint one immediately before the calls
# that need it — don't mint before a build and use it after.

inputs:
  service-account-json:
    description: The PLAY_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON secret, verbatim.
    required: true

outputs:
  token-file:
    description: Path to a file containing the bearer token (no trailing newline).
    value: ${{ steps.mint.outputs.token-file }}
  client-email:
    description: The service account's email, for logging.
    value: ${{ steps.mint.outputs.client-email }}

runs:
  using: composite
  steps:
    - id: mint
      shell: bash
      env:
        SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON: ${{ inputs.service-account-json }}
      run: |
        set -euo pipefail

        if [ -z "${SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON:-}" ]; then
          echo "::error::PLAY_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON secret is empty or unset — set it to the full service-account JSON."
          exit 1
        fi
        sa="$RUNNER_TEMP/sa.json"
        umask 077
        printf '%s' "$SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON" > "$sa"
        if ! jq -e . "$sa" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
          echo "::error::PLAY_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON is not valid JSON."
          exit 1
        fi
        client_email=$(jq -r '.client_email // empty' "$sa")
        token_uri=$(jq -r '.token_uri // "https://oauth2.googleapis.com/token"' "$sa")
        if [ -z "$client_email" ]; then
          echo "::error::Service-account JSON is missing client_email — the secret is malformed."
          exit 1
        fi
        echo "Service account: $client_email"

        # RS256-signed JWT, exchanged for an androidpublisher access token.
        b64url() { openssl base64 -e -A | tr '+/' '-_' | tr -d '='; }
        now=$(date +%s); exp=$((now + 300))
        header_b64=$(printf '%s' '{"alg":"RS256","typ":"JWT"}' | b64url)
        claim_b64=$(jq -nc --arg iss "$client_email" --arg aud "$token_uri" \
          --argjson iat "$now" --argjson exp "$exp" \
          '{iss:$iss, scope:"https://www.googleapis.com/auth/androidpublisher", aud:$aud, iat:$iat, exp:$exp}' \
          | b64url)
        jq -r '.private_key' "$sa" > "$RUNNER_TEMP/key.pem"
        # sa.json holds the same private key as key.pem (which the next few
        # lines already delete once signing is done) but was, until now,
        # never deleted at all -- leaving the full service-account
        # credential readable on disk for the rest of the job, including
        # every Gradle plugin and transitive build dependency that runs
        # after this action returns.
        rm -f "$sa"
        sig=$(printf '%s' "$header_b64.$claim_b64" | openssl dgst -sha256 -sign "$RUNNER_TEMP/key.pem" | b64url)
        jwt="$header_b64.$claim_b64.$sig"
        rm -f "$RUNNER_TEMP/key.pem"

        resp="$RUNNER_TEMP/token.json"
        curl -s -o "$resp" -X POST "$token_uri" \
          --data-urlencode "grant_type=urn:ietf:params:oauth:grant-type:jwt-bearer" \
          --data-urlencode "assertion=$jwt"
        access_token=$(jq -r '.access_token // empty' "$resp")
        if [ -z "$access_token" ]; then
          echo "::error::Could not obtain an access token — $token_uri rejected the service-account credentials."
          echo "Error from the token endpoint: $(jq -r '.error_description // .error // "no detail"' "$resp")"
          exit 1
        fi
        echo "::add-mask::$access_token"

        token_file="$RUNNER_TEMP/play-token"
        printf '%s' "$access_token" > "$token_file"
        chmod 600 "$token_file"
        rm -f "$resp"

        {
          echo "token-file=$token_file"
          echo "client-email=$client_email"
        } >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"


######################################################
# FILE: .github/actions/play-publish/action.yml
######################################################

name: "play-publish"
description: "Publish AAB to Google Play using the repo's hand-rolled API calls. This preserves the original behaviour: uses a short-lived token file and uploads one built bundle into multiple tracks/drafts, then commits the edit."

inputs:
  package-name:
    description: "Application package name"
    required: true
  aab:
    description: "Path to the built .aab (glob allowed)"
    required: true
  token-file:
    description: "Path to a file containing a short-lived Play API token (created by play-api-token action)"
    required: true
  expected-version-code:
    description: "The VERSION_CODE built by Gradle (for an additional sanity check)"
    required: false
    default: ''
  completed-track:
    description: "Track to roll out (completed)"
    required: true
  draft-tracks:
    description: "Space-separated list of tracks to stage the same bundle as drafts"
    required: false
    default: ''
  release-name:
    description: "Name of the release (e.g. version name)"
    required: false
    default: ''
  release-notes:
    description: "Release notes text (will be attached as en-US only)"
    required: false
    default: ''

outputs:
  version-code:
    description: "The versionCode that was uploaded and published/drafted"
    value: ${{ steps.publish.outputs.version-code }}
  rolled-out:
    description: "The completed track that was rolled out (empty if none)"
    value: ${{ steps.publish.outputs.rolled-out }}
  drafted:
    description: "Space-separated list of draft tracks that were staged"
    value: ${{ steps.publish.outputs.drafted }}

runs:
  using: "composite"
  steps:
    - name: Ensure publish script is executable
      shell: bash
      run: |
        chmod +x ./.github/actions/play-publish/publish.sh || true

    - id: publish
      shell: bash
      env:
        PACKAGE_NAME: ${{ inputs.package-name }}
        AAB: ${{ inputs.aab }}
        TOKEN_FILE: ${{ inputs.token-file }}
        EXPECTED_VERSION_CODE: ${{ inputs.expected-version-code }}
        COMPLETED_TRACK: ${{ inputs.completed-track }}
        DRAFT_TRACKS: ${{ inputs.draft-tracks }}
        RELEASE_NAME: ${{ inputs.release-name }}
        RELEASE_NOTES: ${{ inputs.release-notes }}
      run: |
        set -euo pipefail
        out="$RUNNER_TEMP/play-publish-out.json"
        # Run the publisher script; it writes JSON to $out on success.
        ./.github/actions/play-publish/publish.sh "$PACKAGE_NAME" "$AAB" "$TOKEN_FILE" "$EXPECTED_VERSION_CODE" "$COMPLETED_TRACK" "$DRAFT_TRACKS" "$RELEASE_NAME" "$RELEASE_NOTES" "$out"
        jq -r '.versionCode // ""' "$out" | awk '{print "version-code="$0}' >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
        jq -r '.rolledOut // ""' "$out" | awk '{print "rolled-out="$0}' >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
        jq -r '.drafted // [] | join(" ")' "$out" | awk '{print "drafted="$0}' >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"

######################################################
# FILE: .github/actions/verify-android-signature/action.yml
######################################################

name: Verify Android artifact signature
description: >
  Prove that a built APK or AAB is actually signed, and signed by the expected
  certificate, before it goes anywhere. Run this between building and
  publishing.

# Gradle's signing config only attaches when every piece of keystore material
# is present; when something is missing it quietly produces an *unsigned*
# artifact instead of failing. That is how an unsigned APK ends up attached to
# a GitHub Release. This action closes that hole: it inspects the artifact that
# was actually produced and fails the job if it isn't signed by the key we
# expect.

inputs:
  artifact:
    description: Path to the .apk or .aab to check.
    required: true
  expected-sha256:
    description: >
      Expected signing-certificate SHA-256 fingerprint. Pass the value the
      keystore action reported (or the KEYSTORE_SHA256 secret). When empty,
      the artifact is only checked for being signed at all.
    required: false
    default: ''

runs:
  using: composite
  steps:
    - shell: bash
      env:
        ARTIFACT: ${{ inputs.artifact }}
        EXPECTED_SHA256: ${{ inputs.expected-sha256 }}
      run: |
        set -euo pipefail

        if [ ! -f "$ARTIFACT" ]; then
          echo "::error::Nothing to verify — $ARTIFACT does not exist."
          exit 1
        fi

        case "$ARTIFACT" in
          *-unsigned.apk|*-unsigned.aab)
            echo "::error::$ARTIFACT is the unsigned output. The signing config did not attach — check that the keystore secrets are present and reached Gradle."
            exit 1
            ;;
        esac

        norm() { tr -d ': \t' | tr '[:lower:]' '[:upper:]'; }
        actual=""

        if [[ "$ARTIFACT" == *.apk ]]; then
          # apksigner ships with the build-tools; pick the newest installed.
          apksigner=$(find "${ANDROID_HOME:-${ANDROID_SDK_ROOT:-/usr/local/lib/android/sdk}}/build-tools" \
            -maxdepth 2 -name apksigner -type f 2>/dev/null | sort -V | tail -n1 || true)
          if [ -z "$apksigner" ]; then
            echo "::error::apksigner not found in the Android SDK — cannot verify the APK signature."
            exit 1
          fi
          if ! "$apksigner" verify --print-certs "$ARTIFACT" > "$RUNNER_TEMP/verify.txt" 2>&1; then
            echo "::error::$ARTIFACT is not correctly signed."
            cat "$RUNNER_TEMP/verify.txt"
            exit 1
          fi
          cat "$RUNNER_TEMP/verify.txt"
          actual=$(grep -m1 -i 'SHA-256 digest:' "$RUNNER_TEMP/verify.txt" | sed 's/.*digest: *//' | tr -d '\r')
        else
          # An .aab is jar-signed; read the signer certificate straight out of
          # the block file, which also proves the signature block exists.
          block=$(unzip -Z1 "$ARTIFACT" 'META-INF/*.RSA' 'META-INF/*.EC' 'META-INF/*.DSA' 2>/dev/null | head -n1 || true)
          if [ -z "$block" ]; then
            echo "::error::$ARTIFACT contains no signature block — the bundle is unsigned."
            exit 1
          fi
          if ! jarsigner -verify -strict "$ARTIFACT" > "$RUNNER_TEMP/verify.txt" 2>&1; then
            echo "::warning::jarsigner reported warnings for $ARTIFACT:"
            cat "$RUNNER_TEMP/verify.txt"
          fi
          unzip -p "$ARTIFACT" "$block" | keytool -printcert > "$RUNNER_TEMP/cert.txt"
          grep -E '^(Owner|Valid from)|SHA256:' "$RUNNER_TEMP/cert.txt" || true
          actual=$(grep -m1 'SHA256:' "$RUNNER_TEMP/cert.txt" | sed 's/.*SHA256: *//' | tr -d '\r')
        fi

        if [ -z "$actual" ]; then
          echo "::error::Could not read a signing certificate fingerprint out of $ARTIFACT."
          exit 1
        fi

        if [ -n "${EXPECTED_SHA256:-}" ]; then
          if [ "$(printf '%s' "$actual" | norm)" != "$(printf '%s' "$EXPECTED_SHA256" | norm)" ]; then
            echo "::error::$ARTIFACT is signed by the wrong certificate."
            echo "  expected: $EXPECTED_SHA256"
            echo "  actual:   $actual"
            exit 1
          fi
          echo "Signature verified against the expected certificate ($actual)."
        else
          echo "Signed by $actual (no expected fingerprint supplied, so identity was not checked)."
        fi

######################################################
# FILE: .github/workflows/backup-repo.yml
######################################################

name: Backup Repository to TXT

on:
  push:
    branches: ["**"]
  workflow_dispatch:

jobs:
  backup:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest

    permissions:
      contents: write

    steps:
      - name: Checkout repository
        uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with:
          fetch-depth: 0

      - name: Build backup TXT file
        run: |
          BACKUP_FILE="repo_backup.txt"
          REPO_NAME="${{ github.repository }}"
          TIMESTAMP=$(date -u +"%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S UTC")
          COMMIT_SHA="${{ github.sha }}"
          BRANCH="${{ github.ref_name }}"

          echo "======================================================" > "$BACKUP_FILE"
          echo "  REPOSITORY BACKUP" >> "$BACKUP_FILE"
          echo "======================================================" >> "$BACKUP_FILE"
          echo "  Repo:      $REPO_NAME" >> "$BACKUP_FILE"
          echo "  Branch:    $BRANCH" >> "$BACKUP_FILE"
          echo "  Commit:    $COMMIT_SHA" >> "$BACKUP_FILE"
          echo "  Timestamp: $TIMESTAMP" >> "$BACKUP_FILE"
          echo "======================================================" >> "$BACKUP_FILE"
          echo "" >> "$BACKUP_FILE"

          # Find all files, excluding .git and the backup file itself
          find . \
            -type f \
            ! -path "./.git/*" \
            ! -name "$BACKUP_FILE" \
            | sort \
            | while read -r filepath; do

            # Strip leading ./
            clean_path="${filepath#./}"

            echo "" >> "$BACKUP_FILE"
            echo "######################################################" >> "$BACKUP_FILE"
            echo "# FILE: $clean_path" >> "$BACKUP_FILE"
            echo "######################################################" >> "$BACKUP_FILE"
            echo "" >> "$BACKUP_FILE"

            # Check if file is binary
            if file "$filepath" | grep -qE 'binary|executable|ELF|archive|compressed|image|audio|video'; then
              echo "[BINARY FILE - SKIPPED]" >> "$BACKUP_FILE"
            else
              cat "$filepath" >> "$BACKUP_FILE" 2>/dev/null || echo "[COULD NOT READ FILE]" >> "$BACKUP_FILE"
            fi

            echo "" >> "$BACKUP_FILE"
          done

          echo "" >> "$BACKUP_FILE"
          echo "======================================================" >> "$BACKUP_FILE"
          echo "  END OF BACKUP" >> "$BACKUP_FILE"
          echo "======================================================" >> "$BACKUP_FILE"

          echo "Backup complete. Size: $(wc -c < "$BACKUP_FILE") bytes"
      - name: Sanitize branch name
        run: |
          SAFE_BRANCH=$(echo "${{ github.ref_name }}" | tr '/' '-')
          echo "SAFE_BRANCH=$SAFE_BRANCH" >> $GITHUB_ENV

      - name: Sanitize branch name for artifact
        id: sanitize
        run: |
          SAFE_BRANCH=$(echo "${{ github.ref_name }}" | tr '/' '-')
          echo "safe_branch=$SAFE_BRANCH" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"

      - name: Upload backup as artifact
        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        with:
          name: repo-backup-${{ env.SAFE_BRANCH }}-${{ github.sha }}
          path: repo_backup.txt
          retention-days: 90


      - name: Commit backup to repo (optional)
        run: |
          git config user.name "github-actions[bot]"
          git config user.email "github-actions[bot]@users.noreply.github.com"

          # Only commit if there are changes
          if ! git diff --quiet repo_backup.txt 2>/dev/null || ! git ls-files --error-unmatch repo_backup.txt 2>/dev/null; then
            git add repo_backup.txt
            git commit -m "chore: update repo backup [skip ci]"
            git push
          else
            echo "No changes to backup file, skipping commit."
          fi


######################################################
# FILE: .github/workflows/build-apk.yml
######################################################

name: Build and Publish QuicLoc APK

# Two modes:
#   • push / pull_request  -> build a DEBUG APK and upload it as a workflow
#     artifact (CI sanity + a quick downloadable build). No release is created.
#   • workflow_dispatch     -> "Run workflow" button. Builds a SIGNED RELEASE APK
#     (same keystore secrets as the Play workflow) and publishes it to a GitHub
#     Release tagged v<versionName>. Requires the KEYSTORE_RAW / KEYSTORE_PASSWORD
#     / KEY_ALIAS / KEY_PASSWORD secrets (see docs/PLAY_PUBLISHING.md); the job
#     fails rather than publishing anything unsigned if they're missing.
#     Once that release is out, the Play publish runs automatically — see the
#     publish-play job at the bottom.

on:
  push:
    branches:
      - main
  pull_request:
    branches:
      - main
  workflow_dispatch:
    inputs:
      prerelease:
        description: "Mark the GitHub Release as a pre-release"
        type: boolean
        default: false

# Needed by softprops/action-gh-release to create the release. The default
# GITHUB_TOKEN is read-only on many repos, which is why publishing a release
# could fail with a 403 before.
permissions:
  contents: write

jobs:
  build:
    name: Build APK
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest

    steps:
      - name: Checkout Code
        uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with:
          fetch-depth: 0   # full history so versionCode = commit count is correct

      - name: Set up JDK 21
        uses: actions/setup-java@v4
        with:
          java-version: '21'
          distribution: 'temurin'
          cache: gradle

      - name: Grant Execute Permission to Gradle Wrapper
        run: chmod +x gradlew

      # ---- Manual publish path: signed RELEASE APK -------------------------
      - name: Prepare signing keystore
        id: keystore
        if: github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch'
        uses: ./.github/actions/android-keystore
        with:
          keystore-raw: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_RAW }}
          keystore-private: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_PRIVATE }}
          keystore-rsa: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_RSA }}
          keystore-public: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_PUBLIC }}
          keystore-chain: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_CHAIN }}
          keystore-password: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_PASSWORD }}
          key-alias: ${{ secrets.KEY_ALIAS }}
          key-password: ${{ secrets.KEY_PASSWORD }}
          expected-sha256: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_SHA256 }}
          expected-sha1: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_SHA1 }}
          expected-owner: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_OWNER }}

      # Only the AAB, not assembleRelease's plain base-module APK: AGP does
      # not fold dynamic-feature modules (:feature_findmyphone) into
      # assembleRelease's output, only bundletool (reading dist:fusing) does
      # — see .github/actions/build-universal-apk's header comment. Building
      # assembleRelease here used to silently ship the GitHub-Release APK
      # with zero find-my-phone code.
      - name: Build signed Release Bundle
        if: github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch'
        env:
          QUICLOC_KEYSTORE_FILE: ${{ steps.keystore.outputs.keystore-path }}
          QUICLOC_KEYSTORE_TYPE: ${{ steps.keystore.outputs.store-type }}
          QUICLOC_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_PASSWORD }}
          QUICLOC_KEY_ALIAS: ${{ secrets.KEY_ALIAS }}
          QUICLOC_KEY_PASSWORD: ${{ secrets.KEY_PASSWORD }}
          # Fail the build rather than emit app-release-unsigned.aab.
          QUICLOC_REQUIRE_SIGNING: 'true'
        run: ./gradlew bundleRelease

      # ---- CI path (push / pull_request): DEBUG APK artifact only ----------
      - name: Build Debug APK
        if: github.event_name != 'workflow_dispatch'
        run: ./gradlew assembleDebug

      # printVersionName runs AFTER the build, so it reads the version.properties
      # value the build just incremented (i.e. it matches the built APK).
      - name: Get App Version
        run: echo "VERSION_NAME=$(./gradlew -q :app:printVersionName)" >> "$GITHUB_ENV"

      # Fuse :feature_findmyphone into a single installable APK via
      # bundletool's universal mode, so sideload users get the same feature
      # set a Play install would download on demand.
      - name: Build fused universal APK
        if: github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch'
        id: universal-apk
        uses: ./.github/actions/build-universal-apk
        with:
          aab-path: app/build/outputs/bundle/release/app-release.aab
          keystore-path: ${{ steps.keystore.outputs.keystore-path }}
          keystore-password: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_PASSWORD }}
          key-alias: ${{ secrets.KEY_ALIAS }}
          key-password: ${{ secrets.KEY_PASSWORD }}
          output-path: app/build/outputs/QuicLoc-v${{ env.VERSION_NAME }}.apk

      - name: Locate & rename APK
        run: |
          if [ "${{ github.event_name }}" = "workflow_dispatch" ]; then
            SRC="${{ steps.universal-apk.outputs.apk-path }}"
            if [ -z "$SRC" ] || [ ! -f "$SRC" ]; then
              echo "::error::No fused universal APK was produced at '$SRC'."
              exit 1
            fi
            echo "APK_PATH=$SRC" >> "$GITHUB_ENV"
          else
            SRC=app/build/outputs/apk/debug/app-debug.apk
            if [ ! -f "$SRC" ]; then
              echo "::error::No APK found to publish ($SRC)"; exit 1
            fi
            OUT="app/build/outputs/QuicLoc-v${VERSION_NAME}.apk"
            mv "$SRC" "$OUT"
            echo "APK_PATH=$OUT" >> "$GITHUB_ENV"
          fi

      - name: Verify the APK is signed by the expected key
        if: github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch'
        uses: ./.github/actions/verify-android-signature
        with:
          artifact: ${{ env.APK_PATH }}
          expected-sha256: ${{ steps.keystore.outputs.sha256 }}

      - name: Sanitize branch name
        run: |
          SAFE_BRANCH=$(echo "${{ github.ref_name }}" | tr '/' '-')
          echo "SAFE_BRANCH=$SAFE_BRANCH" >> "$GITHUB_ENV"

      - name: Upload APK Artifact
        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        with:
          name: quicloc-apk-${{ env.SAFE_BRANCH }}-${{ github.sha }}
          path: ${{ env.APK_PATH }}
          if-no-files-found: error

      - name: Publish GitHub Release (signed APK)
        if: github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch'
        uses: softprops/action-gh-release@v2
        with:
          tag_name: v${{ env.VERSION_NAME }}
          name: QuicLoc v${{ env.VERSION_NAME }}
          files: ${{ env.APK_PATH }}
          prerelease: ${{ inputs.prerelease }}
          fail_on_unmatched_files: true

  # Automatic Play publish, gated on the GitHub release having actually gone
  # out: `needs: build` means this only starts once the release-publishing job
  # succeeded. Sideload first, Play second, one button.
  #
  # version_code_mode: auto because nothing commits the version bump before this
  # runs — the code is taken from whatever Play already holds, so a stale
  # version.properties can't block the release. The Play workflow commits the
  # number back afterwards.
  publish-play:
    name: Publish to Google Play
    needs: build
    if: github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch'
    permissions:
      contents: write
    uses: ./.github/workflows/release-play.yml
    with:
      publish: true
      version_code_mode: auto
    secrets: inherit


######################################################
# FILE: .github/workflows/ci-cd.yml
######################################################

name: CI/CD

# Two shapes, deliberately separated by whether this run can produce something
# a human installs:
#
#   • push to main / pull_request -> test + build, nothing published. The
#     release build here is UNSIGNED on purpose: fork PRs can't read secrets,
#     and building it still exercises packaging/bundletool.
#   • push of a v* tag            -> test + build a SIGNED release APK and AAB,
#     verify the signature, then attach them to a GitHub Release, and — once
#     that release is out — publish to Google Play automatically. If the
#     keystore secrets are missing or wrong, the job fails; it never falls back
#     to publishing an unsigned artifact.
#
# Signing secrets (see docs/PLAY_PUBLISHING.md): KEYSTORE_RAW, KEYSTORE_PASSWORD,
# KEY_ALIAS, KEY_PASSWORD, plus the optional KEYSTORE_SHA256 / KEYSTORE_SHA1 /
# KEYSTORE_OWNER identity checks.

on:
  push:
    branches: [ main ]
    tags: [ 'v*' ]
  pull_request:
    branches: [ main ]

# Least-privilege default for every job in this workflow — CodeQL's Actions
# query flags jobs with no explicit permissions block, since the implicit
# default GITHUB_TOKEN scope depends on a repo-level setting that can be
# read-write. Only `release` and `publish-play` need more (contents: write,
# to create the GitHub Release / commit the version bump); they already
# declare their own permissions block, which overrides this default for
# just those jobs.
permissions:
  contents: read

# Serializes runs on the same ref so two close-together pushes to main (or
# two tag pushes) can't race each other through the versionCode-dependent
# release/publish path. Non-tag runs cancel their predecessor (a superseded
# main-branch CI run is safe to drop); tag runs never do — a real release
# build in flight should run to completion, not be cancelled by whatever
# triggered next.
concurrency:
  group: ${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.ref }}
  cancel-in-progress: ${{ !startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/v') }}

jobs:
  test:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - name: Set up JDK 21
        uses: actions/setup-java@v4
        with:
          java-version: '21'
          distribution: 'temurin'
      - name: Run unit tests
        run: ./gradlew testDebugUnitTest

  build:
    needs: test
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with:
          fetch-depth: 0
      - name: Set up JDK 21
        uses: actions/setup-java@v4
        with:
          java-version: '21'
          distribution: 'temurin'

      - name: Build Debug APK
        run: ./gradlew assembleDebug

      # ---- Non-tag runs: unsigned, never published ------------------------
      - name: Build Release APK + Bundle (unsigned CI check)
        if: github.ref != 'refs/heads/main' && !startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/v')
        run: ./gradlew assembleRelease bundleRelease
        # Unsigned by design — no keystore secrets are wired in here, and fork
        # PRs couldn't read them anyway. This still exercises
        # :app:packageReleaseBundle / bundletool, so dynamic-feature
        # bundle-merge conflicts (e.g. clashing META-INF/*.version metadata
        # between :app and :feature_findmyphone) are caught in PR CI rather
        # than at release time. Nothing from this step is ever published.

      # ---- Tag runs: signed, and proven signed before publishing ----------
      - name: Prepare signing keystore
        id: keystore
        if: github.ref == 'refs/heads/main' || startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/v')
        uses: ./.github/actions/android-keystore
        with:
          keystore-raw: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_RAW }}
          keystore-private: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_PRIVATE }}
          keystore-rsa: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_RSA }}
          keystore-public: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_PUBLIC }}
          keystore-chain: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_CHAIN }}
          keystore-password: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_PASSWORD }}
          key-alias: ${{ secrets.KEY_ALIAS }}
          key-password: ${{ secrets.KEY_PASSWORD }}
          expected-sha256: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_SHA256 }}
          expected-sha1: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_SHA1 }}
          expected-owner: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_OWNER }}

      - name: Build signed Release Bundle
        if: github.ref == 'refs/heads/main' || startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/v')
        env:
          QUICLOC_KEYSTORE_FILE: ${{ steps.keystore.outputs.keystore-path }}
          QUICLOC_KEYSTORE_TYPE: ${{ steps.keystore.outputs.store-type }}
          QUICLOC_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_PASSWORD }}
          QUICLOC_KEY_ALIAS: ${{ secrets.KEY_ALIAS }}
          QUICLOC_KEY_PASSWORD: ${{ secrets.KEY_PASSWORD }}
          QUICLOC_REQUIRE_SIGNING: 'true'
        # Only the AAB, not assembleRelease's plain base-module APK: that APK
        # never contained :feature_findmyphone (AGP doesn't fold dynamic
        # feature modules into `assembleRelease`, only bundletool reads
        # dist:fusing — see the build-universal-apk action below), so it was
        # the wrong artifact to publish as the sideload download.
        run: ./gradlew bundleRelease

      - name: Collect signed AAB
        if: github.ref == 'refs/heads/main' || startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/v')
        id: collect-aab
        run: |
          set -euo pipefail
          mkdir -p signed
          aab=$(find app/build/outputs/bundle/release -name "*.aab" | head -n1)
          if [ -z "$aab" ]; then
            echo "::error::Expected a signed AAB. Found none."
            find app/build/outputs -name "*.aab" || true
            exit 1
          fi
          version=$(./gradlew -q :app:printVersionName)
          cp "$aab" "signed/QuicLoc-v${version}.aab"
          echo "SIGNED_AAB=signed/QuicLoc-v${version}.aab" >> "$GITHUB_ENV"
          echo "VERSION_NAME=$version" >> "$GITHUB_ENV"

      # The sideload APK attached to the GitHub Release must actually contain
      # find-my-phone (fused via dist:fusing), which assembleRelease alone
      # cannot produce — see the action's own header comment.
      - name: Build fused universal APK (sideload download)
        if: github.ref == 'refs/heads/main' || startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/v')
        id: universal-apk
        uses: ./.github/actions/build-universal-apk
        with:
          aab-path: ${{ env.SIGNED_AAB }}
          keystore-path: ${{ steps.keystore.outputs.keystore-path }}
          keystore-password: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_PASSWORD }}
          key-alias: ${{ secrets.KEY_ALIAS }}
          key-password: ${{ secrets.KEY_PASSWORD }}
          output-path: signed/QuicLoc-v${{ env.VERSION_NAME }}.apk

      - name: Verify APK signature
        if: github.ref == 'refs/heads/main' || startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/v')
        uses: ./.github/actions/verify-android-signature
        with:
          artifact: ${{ steps.universal-apk.outputs.apk-path }}
          expected-sha256: ${{ steps.keystore.outputs.sha256 }}

      - name: Verify bundle signature
        if: github.ref == 'refs/heads/main' || startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/v')
        uses: ./.github/actions/verify-android-signature
        with:
          artifact: ${{ env.SIGNED_AAB }}
          expected-sha256: ${{ steps.keystore.outputs.sha256 }}

      - name: Upload debug artifact
        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        with:
          name: apks
          path: app/build/outputs/apk/debug/*.apk

      # Only the verified, signed artifacts reach the release job — the debug
      # APK is deliberately not among them. It carries Android's shared debug
      # signature, so shipping it alongside a real release gives users an APK
      # they can install but never upgrade from.
      - name: Upload signed release artifacts
        if: github.ref == 'refs/heads/main' || startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/v')
        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        with:
          name: signed-release
          path: signed/*
          if-no-files-found: error

  release:
    if: startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/v')
    needs: build
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    permissions:
      contents: write
    steps:
      - name: Download signed artifacts
        uses: actions/download-artifact@v4
        with:
          name: signed-release
          path: signed
      - name: Create Release
        uses: softprops/action-gh-release@v2
        with:
          files: signed/*
          fail_on_unmatched_files: true

  # Play publish is tag-only, same as `release` above, and genuinely follows
  # it (needs: [build, release], not just build) rather than racing it: a
  # plain push to main only builds and tests, it was never meant to burn a
  # versionCode and roll out a release with no corresponding GitHub Release
  # to point back to. See the header comment at the top of this file.
  publish-play:
    if: startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/v')
    needs: [ build, release ]
    permissions:
      contents: write
    uses: ./.github/workflows/release-play.yml
    with:
      publish: true
      version_code_mode: auto
    secrets: inherit


######################################################
# FILE: .github/workflows/deploy-sftp.yml
######################################################

name: Deploy Webpage over FTP

on:
  push:
    branches: [ main, master ]
  workflow_dispatch:

# This workflow only checks out the repo and mirrors docs/ over SFTP — no
# GitHub API calls, no releases, no commits. Least-privilege explicit scope
# rather than relying on the repo's default GITHUB_TOKEN permissions.
permissions:
  contents: read

concurrency:
  group: sftp-deploy
  cancel-in-progress: true

jobs:
  deploy:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - name: Check out source
        uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - name: Deploy via lftp (SFTP)
        env:
          HOST: ${{ secrets.FTP_SERVER }}
          USERNAME: ${{ secrets.FTP_USERNAME }}
          PASSWORD: ${{ secrets.FTP_PASSWORD }}
          PORT: ${{ vars.SFTP_PORT || '22' }}
          REMOTE_DIR: '/quicloc'
          # Optional: the server's known_hosts-format SSH host key line
          # (e.g. the output of `ssh-keyscan -p <port> <host>`), so the host
          # key can be pinned instead of blindly trusted. Not set yet on
          # this repo -- see the warning this step prints until it is.
          SFTP_HOST_KEY: ${{ secrets.SFTP_HOST_KEY }}
        run: |
          set -e
          sudo apt-get install -y lftp

          USER_TRIMMED=$(printf '%s' "$USERNAME" | tr -d ' \t\r\n')
          PW_TRIMMED=$(printf '%s' "$PASSWORD" | tr -d '\r\n')

          # sftp:auto-confirm yes accepts ANY host key on first connection
          # with no verification -- a DNS hijack or network-position MITM
          # gets silently trusted and handed the FTP credentials. Pin the
          # real host key via the SFTP_HOST_KEY secret when it's set
          # (`ssh-keyscan -p "$PORT" "$HOST"` run once, out of band, to
          # obtain it); until then, fall back to the old trust-on-first-use
          # behavior but say so loudly instead of silently.
          if [ -n "$SFTP_HOST_KEY" ]; then
            known_hosts="$RUNNER_TEMP/sftp_known_hosts"
            printf '%s\n' "$SFTP_HOST_KEY" > "$known_hosts"
            AUTO_CONFIRM=no
            SET_KNOWN_HOSTS="set sftp:known-hosts-files \"$known_hosts\";"
          else
            echo "::warning::SFTP_HOST_KEY secret is not set -- the deploy host's SSH key is trusted on first use with no verification (sftp:auto-confirm). Run 'ssh-keyscan -p $PORT $HOST' once and store the output as the SFTP_HOST_KEY secret to close this."
            AUTO_CONFIRM=yes
            SET_KNOWN_HOSTS=""
          fi

          # Mirror the docs directory to /quicloc. No `ssl:verify-certificate`
          # setting here -- SFTP runs over SSH, not TLS, so that option
          # (a leftover from FTPS-flavored lftp configs) doesn't apply to
          # this sftp:// connection and disabling it bought nothing.
          lftp -u "$USER_TRIMMED","$PW_TRIMMED" \
            -p "$PORT" \
            -e "$SET_KNOWN_HOSTS set sftp:auto-confirm $AUTO_CONFIRM; mirror -R --verbose ./docs/ $REMOTE_DIR; quit" \
            sftp://"$HOST"


######################################################
# FILE: .github/workflows/release-play.yml
######################################################

name: Release to Play

# Signed Android App Bundle build + Google Play publish.
#
# One build, one upload, every track: the bundle is rolled out (status
# "completed") on internal testing, and the *same* versionCode is staged as a
# draft on closed testing, open testing and production. Promoting any of those
# drafts later ships the exact artifact internal testers used — no rebuild, no
# re-sign, no second versionCode.
#
# Nothing reaches the public: drafts sit in the console until a human rolls them
# out. `publish: false` builds and uploads the artifact here without touching
# Play at all.
#
# Required repo secrets (see docs/PLAY_PUBLISHING.md):
#   KEYSTORE_RAW               - base64 of the upload keystore (.jks / .p12)
#   KEYSTORE_PASSWORD          - keystore (store) password
#   KEY_ALIAS                  - key alias
#   KEY_PASSWORD               - key password
#   PLAY_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON  - Google Play service-account JSON (publish only)
#
# Optional, used to prove the right key was loaded before anything is signed:
#   KEYSTORE_SHA256 / KEYSTORE_SHA1 / KEYSTORE_OWNER
# Optional, used to rebuild the keystore if KEYSTORE_RAW isn't set:
#   KEYSTORE_PRIVATE / KEYSTORE_RSA / KEYSTORE_PUBLIC / KEYSTORE_CHAIN

on:
  # Called by the workflows that publish a GitHub release, so a Play publish
  # follows automatically once the release is out. Also runnable by hand.
  workflow_call:
    inputs:
      publish:
        type: boolean
        default: true
      completed_track:
        type: string
        default: internal
      draft_tracks:
        type: string
        default: alpha beta production
      release_notes:
        type: string
        default: ''
      version_code_mode:
        type: string
        default: auto
  workflow_dispatch:
    inputs:
      publish:
        description: "Publish to Play (off = build the .aab and upload it as a workflow artifact only)"
        type: boolean
        default: true
      completed_track:
        description: "Track to actually roll out to"
        type: choice
        options: [internal, alpha, beta, production]
        default: internal
      draft_tracks:
        description: "Tracks to stage the same bundle in as a draft (space-separated)"
        type: string
        default: alpha beta production
      release_notes:
        description: "Optional en-US release notes attached to every track's release"
        type: string
        default: ''
      version_code_mode:
        description: >
          How to pick the versionCode. auto = next after whatever Play already
          holds, so a stale version.properties can never block a release;
          strict = use version.properties exactly and fail if Play is ahead.
        type: choice
        options: [auto, strict]
        default: auto

jobs:
  build-publish:
    name: Build signed AAB${{ inputs.publish && ' + publish' || '' }}
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    # contents: write so the consumed versionCode can be committed back — see
    # the last step. Everything else here only talks to Google.
    permissions:
      contents: write
    steps:
      - name: Checkout
        uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with:
          fetch-depth: 0   # full history so versionCode = commit count is correct

      - name: Set up JDK 21
        uses: actions/setup-java@v4
        with:
          java-version: '21'
          distribution: 'temurin'
          cache: gradle

      - name: Grant execute permission to Gradle wrapper
        run: chmod +x gradlew

      - name: Prepare signing keystore
        id: keystore
        uses: ./.github/actions/android-keystore
        with:
          keystore-raw: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_RAW }}
          keystore-private: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_PRIVATE }}
          keystore-rsa: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_RSA }}
          keystore-public: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_PUBLIC }}
          keystore-chain: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_CHAIN }}
          keystore-password: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_PASSWORD }}
          key-alias: ${{ secrets.KEY_ALIAS }}
          key-password: ${{ secrets.KEY_PASSWORD }}
          expected-sha256: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_SHA256 }}
          expected-sha1: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_SHA1 }}
          expected-owner: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_OWNER }}

      - name: Read application id
        run: echo "APP_ID=$(./gradlew -q :app:printApplicationId)" >> "$GITHUB_ENV"

      # Everything Play-related is hand-rolled against the API (curl + jq +
      # openssl, all on ubuntu-latest) rather than delegated to a third-party
      # action: it is the only way to get one upload into several tracks, and it
      # means the signing key and the service account never leave this repo's
      # own actions.
      - name: Mint a Play API token (preflight)
        id: preflight-token
        if: ${{ inputs.publish }}
        uses: ./.github/actions/play-api-token
        with:
          service-account-json: ${{ secrets.PLAY_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON }}

      # Fail early and legibly. r0adkll/upload-google-play used to report every
      # API problem as "Unknown error occurred."; opening an edit here surfaces
      # the real status instead:
      #   401 -> bad/blocked credentials
      #   403 -> service account lacks permission, or the Play Developer API
      #          isn't enabled for its project
      #   404 -> app/package not found, i.e. no manual first release exists yet
      # It also reads back the highest versionCode Play holds, which is the
      # number the next step needs.
      - name: Preflight Google Play API access
        if: ${{ inputs.publish }}
        run: |
          set -euo pipefail
          token=$(cat "${{ steps.preflight-token.outputs.token-file }}")
          api="https://androidpublisher.googleapis.com/androidpublisher/v3/applications/${APP_ID}"
          echo "Package: $APP_ID"

          resp="$RUNNER_TEMP/edit.json"
          http=$(curl -sS -o "$resp" -w "%{http_code}" -X POST -H "Authorization: Bearer $token" "$api/edits")
          echo "Play API edits.insert -> HTTP $http"
          echo "Response body:"; cat "$resp"; echo
          if [ "$http" != "200" ] && [ "$http" != "201" ]; then
            echo "::error::Play Developer API rejected the request (HTTP $http). See the response body above for the exact cause (403=permission/API not enabled, 404=app or first release missing, 401=bad credentials)."
            exit 1
          fi
          edit_id=$(jq -r '.id // empty' "$resp")

          # While the edit is open, ask Play what it already holds. A versionCode
          # at or below the highest existing one uploads fine and then fails at
          # rollout with "does not allow any existing users to upgrade" — an
          # error that says nothing about version codes and is easy to misread.
          # Better to know the number now than to find out in the console.
          play_max=0
          for kind in bundles apks; do
            list="$RUNNER_TEMP/$kind.json"
            lh=$(curl -sS -o "$list" -w "%{http_code}" \
              -H "Authorization: Bearer $token" "$api/edits/${edit_id}/${kind}")
            if [ "$lh" = "200" ]; then
              m=$(jq -r "[.${kind}[]?.versionCode // 0] | max // 0" "$list")
              [ "$m" -gt "$play_max" ] && play_max=$m
            else
              echo "::warning::Could not list $kind (HTTP $lh) — that source won't be considered."
            fi
          done

          if [ -n "$edit_id" ]; then
            curl -sS -o /dev/null -X DELETE \
              -H "Authorization: Bearer $token" "$api/edits/${edit_id}" || true
          fi
          echo "Highest versionCode already on Play: $play_max"
          echo "PLAY_MAX_VERSION_CODE=$play_max" >> "$GITHUB_ENV"
          echo "Play API access OK — proceeding to build."

      # Gradle increments VERSION_D during the build, so the code this run
      # would produce is the committed value + 1. If that can't clear what Play
      # already holds, the release is dead on arrival — Play accepts the upload
      # and then refuses the rollout.
      #
      # The counter drifts by design: the build increments version.properties on
      # the runner and nothing commits it back, so the file is stale the moment
      # anything ships. `auto` therefore treats Play as the authority and takes
      # the next code after whatever it holds, which cannot collide no matter
      # how far behind the file is. The last step in this job commits the number
      # back so the repo converges with reality.
      - name: Resolve the versionCode
        if: ${{ inputs.publish }}
        run: |
          set -euo pipefail
          committed=$(grep -E '^VERSION_D=' app/version.properties | cut -d= -f2 | tr -d '[:space:]')
          play_max=${PLAY_MAX_VERSION_CODE:-0}
          next=$((committed + 1))
          echo "version.properties VERSION_D=$committed -> would build $next"
          echo "Play's highest versionCode: $play_max"

          if [ "$next" -gt "$play_max" ]; then
            echo "$next clears Play. Building that."
            exit 0
          fi

          if [ "${{ inputs.version_code_mode }}" = "strict" ]; then
            echo "::error::versionCode $next would not clear Play, which already has $play_max. Set VERSION_D=$play_max in app/version.properties and commit (the build adds 1), or re-run in auto mode."
            exit 1
          fi

          # Play is ahead of the file — take the next code after Play's.
          sed -i "s/^VERSION_D=.*/VERSION_D=$play_max/" app/version.properties
          echo "::notice::version.properties was behind Play ($committed vs $play_max). Building versionCode $((play_max + 1)) instead; it will be committed back after publishing."

      - name: Build signed release bundle
        env:
          QUICLOC_KEYSTORE_FILE: ${{ steps.keystore.outputs.keystore-path }}
          QUICLOC_KEYSTORE_TYPE: ${{ steps.keystore.outputs.store-type }}
          QUICLOC_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD: ${{ secrets.KEYSTORE_PASSWORD }}
          QUICLOC_KEY_ALIAS: ${{ secrets.KEY_ALIAS }}
          QUICLOC_KEY_PASSWORD: ${{ secrets.KEY_PASSWORD }}
          # Play rejects an unsigned bundle anyway; fail here with a clear
          # message instead of at upload time with an opaque one.
          QUICLOC_REQUIRE_SIGNING: 'true'
        run: ./gradlew bundleRelease

      - name: Verify the bundle is signed by the expected key
        run: |
          set -euo pipefail
          aab=$(find app/build/outputs/bundle/release -name "*.aab" | head -n1)
          [ -n "$aab" ] || { echo "::error::No .aab was produced."; exit 1; }
          echo "AAB_PATH=$aab" >> "$GITHUB_ENV"

      - name: Check bundle signature
        uses: ./.github/actions/verify-android-signature
        with:
          artifact: ${{ env.AAB_PATH }}
          expected-sha256: ${{ steps.keystore.outputs.sha256 }}

      # Read AFTER bundleRelease so it reflects the version.properties value the
      # build just incremented (matches the .aab's versionName/versionCode).
      - name: Get app version
        run: |
          echo "VERSION_NAME=$(./gradlew -q :app:printVersionName)" >> "$GITHUB_ENV"
          echo "VERSION_CODE=$(./gradlew -q :app:printVersionCode)" >> "$GITHUB_ENV"

      # The pre-build check used the committed VERSION_D; this one uses what was
      # actually built. Belt and braces, because the cost of getting it wrong is
      # a rejected rollout and a burned versionCode.
      - name: Confirm the built versionCode clears Play
        if: ${{ inputs.publish }}
        run: |
          set -euo pipefail
          play_max=${PLAY_MAX_VERSION_CODE:-0}
          echo "Built versionCode $VERSION_CODE; Play's highest is $play_max."
          if [ "$VERSION_CODE" -le "$play_max" ]; then
            echo "::error::The built bundle is versionCode $VERSION_CODE but Play already has $play_max. Refusing to upload — it could not be rolled out to any existing user. Set VERSION_D=$play_max in app/version.properties and commit."
            exit 1
          fi

      - name: Upload AAB artifact
        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        with:
          name: quicloc-release-aab-${{ env.VERSION_NAME }}
          path: |
            app/build/outputs/bundle/release/*.aab
            app/version.properties
          if-no-files-found: error

      # Minted here rather than reused from the preflight: tokens last 5 minutes
      # and the build sits in between.
      - name: Mint a Play API token (publish)
        id: publish-token
        if: ${{ inputs.publish }}
        uses: ./.github/actions/play-api-token
        with:
          service-account-json: ${{ secrets.PLAY_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON }}

      - name: Publish to Google Play
        id: play
        if: ${{ inputs.publish }}
        uses: ./.github/actions/play-publish
        with:
          package-name: ${{ env.APP_ID }}
          aab: ${{ env.AAB_PATH }}
          token-file: ${{ steps.publish-token.outputs.token-file }}
          expected-version-code: ${{ env.VERSION_CODE }}
          completed-track: ${{ inputs.completed_track }}
          draft-tracks: ${{ inputs.draft_tracks }}
          release-name: ${{ env.VERSION_NAME }}
          release-notes: ${{ inputs.release_notes }}

      - name: Say what landed where
        if: ${{ inputs.publish }}
        run: |
          echo "versionCode ${{ steps.play.outputs.version-code }} is live on the ${{ steps.play.outputs.rolled-out }} track."
          drafted="${{ steps.play.outputs.drafted }}"
          if [ -n "$drafted" ]; then
            echo "Drafts staged (same bundle) on: $drafted"
            echo "Promote one in the console when you're ready — no rebuild needed."
          else
            echo "::warning::No draft tracks were set. Check the warnings above for which ones Play refused."
          fi

      # The versionCode is now spent, but the increment only ever happened on
      # this runner. Commit it so the repo tracks what actually shipped and the
      # GitHub release tag (v<versionName>) keeps advancing too.
      #
      # Deliberately non-fatal: the bundle is already published, so a protected
      # branch or a push race must not turn a successful release into a red run.
      # If this fails, the next run's auto resolution reads the number from Play
      # again and still can't collide — the commit is convergence, not
      # correctness.
      - name: Commit the versionCode that shipped
        if: ${{ inputs.publish && steps.play.outputs.version-code != '' }}
        continue-on-error: true
        run: |
          set -uo pipefail
          published="${{ steps.play.outputs.version-code }}"
          for attempt in 1 2 3; do
            git fetch origin main --quiet || true
            # The build mutated version.properties on disk; start from main.
            git reset --hard origin/main --quiet
            current=$(grep -E '^VERSION_D=' app/version.properties | cut -d= -f2 | tr -d '[:space:]')
            if [ "$current" -ge "$published" ]; then
              echo "main already has VERSION_D=$current (>= $published) — nothing to commit."
              exit 0
            fi
            sed -i "s/^VERSION_D=.*/VERSION_D=$published/" app/version.properties
            git -c user.name="github-actions[bot]" \
                -c user.email="41898282+github-actions[bot]@users.noreply.github.com" \
                commit -q -am "chore: versionCode $published shipped to Play [skip ci]"
            if git push --quiet origin HEAD:main; then
              echo "Committed VERSION_D=$published to main."
              exit 0
            fi
            echo "Push attempt $attempt failed (main probably moved) — retrying."
            sleep $((attempt * 3))
          done
          echo "::warning::Published versionCode $published but could not commit the bump to main. Set VERSION_D=$published in app/version.properties by hand. Releases still work without it — auto mode reads the next code from Play."
          {
            echo "### Commit this by hand"
            echo ""
            echo "versionCode **$published** shipped, but the bump could not be pushed to \`main\`."
            echo "Set \`VERSION_D=$published\` in \`app/version.properties\`."
          } >> "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"
